# A5 — Package & Lint Gatekeeper

**Agent:** A5
**Wave:** 1
**Status:** ⚠️ partial
**Lane used:** lint-gate / package-build-gate
**Files changed:** `docs/phase2/handoffs/A5.md` (this file only)
**Files NOT changed (owned by others):**
`packages/studio/src/components/VideoStudio.jsx`, `src/shared/personalization/**`, `src/shared/crossStudio.ts`,
`components/StandaloneShell.js`, `components/SocialPublishProvider.tsx`, `components/AiAssistantProvider.tsx`,
root `package.json`, lock files, `netlify.toml`, `.github/`, `docs/` (other than this handoff), `e2e/`.

---

## TL;DR

| Gate | Result |
|---|---|
| **Gate 1 — `npm run lint`** | **RUN. Exit 1. 1719 findings (1224 errors + 495 warnings) across 251 files.** Exactly **2** findings were introduced by the Phase 1 diff; **0 new errors**. The other **1717 are pre-existing at the pinned baseline** (proven, not asserted). |
| **Gate 2 — `npm run build:packages`** | **NOT RUN — build lock held by A1 for the entire window.** Determined analytically + by workspace-resolution probe that step 1 (`build:workflow`) cannot run in this worktree for a **pre-existing environment reason** (uninitialised git submodules), and that the Phase 1 import does **not** break the studio Babel build (Babel does not resolve imports). The orchestrator must run the build. |
| **Cross-package import** | **NOT sound.** `packages/studio` is published standalone and its published `src/` now contains a path that only exists inside this repo. **Recommend: move the button to the app root and inject it via a render prop (A11).** |

```

**Interpretation**

* 251 files, **1719 findings** = **1224 errors** + **495 warnings**.
* `next lint` in Next 15.5.15 lints only the default directories `app`, `pages`, `components`, `lib`, `src`.
  It does **not** lint `packages/`, `e2e/`, `tests/`, `scripts/` or `electron/`. This is a **scope gap of the
  default Next.js lint scope**, not a broken config — so the Phase 1 change to
  `packages/studio/src/components/VideoStudio.jsx` and the new `e2e/personalization-step4-remotion-designs.spec.ts`
  were never linted at all. (Empirically confirmed: neither file appears anywhere in the output.)
* The lint config itself is **not broken**: `.eslintrc.json` is `{ "extends": ["next/core-web-vitals", "next/typescript"] }`.
  `eslint-config-next@15.5.27` ships `core-web-vitals.js` and `typescript.js`; `typescript.js` extends
  `plugin:@typescript-eslint/recommended` and sets `'@typescript-eslint/no-unused-vars': 1`. The observed output
  matches that design exactly (`no-explicit-any` → Error, `no-unused-vars` → Warning). **I did not edit the eslint config.**

---

## Lint finding classification

### (a) Introduced by the Phase 1 diff — **2 findings, both Warnings, 0 new Errors**

| # | File | Line | Rule | Message |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| a1 | `src/shared/personalization/DemoPersonalizeProvider.tsx` | `1354:5` | `react-hooks/exhaustive-deps` | `React Hook useMemo has an unnecessary dependency: 'remotionDesignRegistryVersion'. Either exclude it or remove the dependency array.` |
| a2 | `src/shared/personalization/preview/__tests__/designLayerComposition.test.tsx` | `30:40` | `@next/next/no-img-element` | `Using <img> could result in slower LCP and higher bandwidth…` (new file created by Phase 1) |

Source of a1 — `src/shared/personalization/DemoPersonalizeProvider.tsx:1350-1356`:

```jsx
  const remotionDesignTemplates = useMemo(
    () => listRemotionDesignTemplates(),
    [remotionDesignRegistryVersion],
  )
```

`listRemotionDesignTemplates()` is a plain module-level read; the version counter is a *cache-busting* signal, not a
value the memo body consumes, so ESLint is right that it is an "unnecessary dependency" by the rule's definition.
It is a **Warning**, does not fail the build, and the *intent* (re-derive the list when the registry changes) is
correct — the fix would be to read the version inside the memo body, e.g.
`() => { void remotionDesignRegistryVersion; return listRemotionDesignTemplates() }`. **Out of my ownership**
(`src/shared/personalization/**` is owned by other agents) and **not a build blocker** → I did not change it.

Source of a2 — `src/shared/personalization/preview/__tests__/designLayerComposition.test.tsx:30`:

```jsx
    Img: ({ src }: { src: string }) => <img src={src} alt="" />,
```

This is a `vi.mock('remotion', …)` stub of Remotion's `Img` component inside a **test file**. `@next/next/no-img-element`
does not apply to a mocked Remotion primitive. **Out of my ownership** and a **Warning only** → I did not change it.

### (b) Pre-existing at the pinned baseline — **1717 findings (1224 errors + 493 warnings)**

**Proven, not asserted.** The Phase 1 diff is *committed* (HEAD == `84a9d1586`, working tree clean of source changes
at lint time), so `git stash push -u` has nothing to stash. I therefore reproduced the baseline in an **isolated
pristine tree** instead of mutating this worktree — same result, zero risk to the branch:

```
$ git archive 0bfecda0f | tar -x -C /tmp/kilo/a5-baseline
$ ln -s <repo>/node_modules /tmp/kilo/a5-baseline/node_modules
$ cd /tmp/kilo/a5-baseline && node node_modules/next/dist/bin/next lint
```

To rule out contamination by other agents' in-flight edits, I ran the **same isolated-tree procedure against HEAD
(84a9d1586)** and diffed the two. The diff below is machine-generated, per-file, rule+message normalised
(line numbers ignored) so that pure line-shifts are not reported as changes.

```
BASELINE 0bfecda0f : files=250 findings=1717 errors=1224 warnings=493
HEAD     84a9d1586 : files=251 findings=1719 errors=1224 warnings=495
DELTA              : files=+1 findings=+2 errors=+0 warnings=+2

=== files whose finding SET (rule+message, ignoring line numbers) differs ===

### src/shared/personalization/DemoPersonalizeProvider.tsx  [CHANGED]
   ADDED  : Warning | React Hook useMemo has an unnecessary dependency: 'remotionDesignRegistryVersion'. Either exclude it or remove the dependency array.  | react-hooks/exhaustive-deps

### src/shared/personalization/preview/__tests__/designLayerComposition.test.tsx  [NEW FILE]
   ADDED  : Warning | Using `<img>` could result in slower LCP and higher bandwidth. Consider using `<Image />` from `next/image` or a custom image loader to automatically optimize images. This may incur additional usage or cost from your provider. See: https://nextjs.org/docs/messages/no-img-element  | @next/next/no-img-element

=== identical-finding files (count) ===
248 of 251 files have an IDENTICAL finding set (line numbers may shift)
```

The only other two files that differ are **pure line-number shifts** caused by Phase 1 inserting lines above them
(`guided/OverlayEditor.tsx` 307→326 & 363→384, `guided/StepPersonalize.tsx` 122→123) — same rule, same message.

**Net effect of the Phase 1 diff on lint: +0 errors, +2 warnings, +1 new file.**

### (c) False positives from a broken lint config — **0**

The eslint config is correct and functioning (see Gate 1 interpretation above). No finding in the output is a
config artifact. The 1224 errors are a **codebase** condition — the repo has evidently never had a clean
`npm run lint` — not a config defect, so per my ownership rules I did **not** weaken the config to make the gate green.

> **Recommendation for the orchestrator (not a Wave-1 blocker):** `npm run lint` has been red repo-wide since before
> Phase 1 (1224 errors at the pinned baseline). It is not wired into `.github/workflows/` at all
> (`grep -rn lint .github/workflows/` → no hits), which is why the breakage went unnoticed. Two separate work items:
> (i) decide whether `next/typescript`'s `no-explicit-any: error` is the severity the team wants (it alone accounts for
> the large majority of the 1224) and, if not, downgrade it to a warning in `.eslintrc.json`; and
> (ii) add `packages/`, `e2e/` and `tests/` to the lint scope so package code is actually covered.
> Neither is mine to change.

## Gate 2 — `npm run build:packages`

### Status: **NOT RUN — the build lock was held by A1 for the whole window.**

`docs/phase2/BUILD_LOCK` at the start of my run and at the end:

```
**Status:** GRANTED
**Holder:** A1 (Export Evidence Engineer)
**Granted at:** 2026-10-20, Wave 1
**Released at:** —
```

I polled the lock file every 20 s from `00:05:12Z` to `00:16:54Z` (36 consecutive reads) and again from
`00:19Z` onwards; it never read `FREE`. I did **not** run any build command while it was held, and I did not run a
build concurrently with A1, because `packages/studio/dist` is **tracked in git** (§3) and a concurrent build would
have written over A1's artifacts.

Per the brief's instruction ("if it is still held after 25 minutes, write the handoff with the lint results and a
clear 'build not run: lock held' status, and the orchestrator will run it"), the build is left to the orchestrator.

### What I *could* determine without the lock (deterministic, non-build probes)

**(i) `build:packages` = `build:workflow && build:agent && build:studio`. Steps 1 and 2 cannot run in this worktree
at all — for a pre-existing environment reason, not a code reason.**

`packages/Vibe-Workflow` and `packages/Open-Poe-AI` are **git submodules that are not checked out in this worktree**:

```
$ git submodule status
-3e21ebc92d93bd699ffc6000bbcf980eaa8830cb packages/Open-Poe-AI
-210da54714902983e2c6fdedfad88fecacac553b packages/Vibe-Workflow

$ ls packages/Vibe-Workflow/ packages/Open-Poe-AI/
packages/Open-Poe-AI:   (empty)
packages/Vibe-Workflow: (empty)
```

So npm cannot resolve those two workspaces by name. Non-build probe (`npm run env` resolves the workspace but runs
no script):

```
$ npm run env -w workflow-builder
npm error No workspaces found:
npm error   --workspace=workflow-builder
A complete log of this run can be found in: /Users/deanellgilmore/.npm/_logs/2026-10-21T00_04_55_144Z-debug-0.log

$ npm run env -w ai-agent
npm error No workspaces found:
npm error   --workspace=ai-agent
A complete log of this run can be found in: /Users/deanellgilmore/.npm/_logs/2026-10-21T00_04_56_139Z-debug-0.log
```

`npm run build -w workflow-builder` fails in npm's workspace-resolution phase, i.e. **before** any build script
starts, with exactly that error. `build:packages` chains with `&&`, so it will stop at step 1 and
**`build:studio` will never be reached**.

**This is provably not caused by the Phase 1 diff.** The Phase 1 diff is 42 files and touches **no** `package.json`
and **no** submodule gitlink — the full file list is:

```

The submodule gitlinks are byte-identical at the pinned baseline (`0bfecda0f`) and at HEAD, and the failure occurs
before any build script executes. **Verdict: pre-existing environment condition, not a Phase 1 regression.**

> **To make the gate meaningful the orchestrator should run, in this worktree:**
> `git submodule update --init --recursive` (the repo's own `setup` script does exactly this), then
> `npm run build:packages`. I deliberately did **not** initialise the submodules myself: it is a large network
> fetch that mutates the shared worktree, and it could have changed the outcome of A1's in-flight build.

**(ii) The Phase 1 import does NOT break the studio Babel build.**

`packages/studio` builds with:

```
"build": "npm run build:css && babel src --out-dir dist --extensions .js,.jsx"
```

and `packages/studio/babel.config.json` is:

```json
{
  "presets": [
    "@babel/preset-env",
    ["@babel/preset-react", { "runtime": "automatic" }]
  ]
}
```

Babel transpiles each file **individually** and **never resolves, verifies or bundles import targets**. There is no
module-resolver plugin and no alias configuration. Therefore:

* `babel src --out-dir dist --extensions .js,.jsx` will happily transpile `packages/studio/src/components/VideoStudio.jsx`
  and emit a `require("../../../../src/shared/personalization/studioEntry")` into `dist/components/VideoStudio.js`.
* The target `src/shared/personalization/studioEntry.ts` is a **TypeScript** file, is **outside** `packages/studio/src`,
  and `.ts` is **not** in the `--extensions` list — so it is neither compiled nor copied into `dist/`.
* **The build itself will therefore succeed.** The breakage is at *publish/runtime* level, not at build level. See §3.

**(iii) The import depth is correct.** Verified by resolving the specifier from its own directory:

```
$ python3 -c "import os; print(os.path.abspath('packages/studio/src/components/../../../../src/shared/personalization/studioEntry'))"
/Users/deanellgilmore/Downloads/openbolt/OpenHiggsBolt/.kilo/worktrees/personalization-upgrades/src/shared/personalization/studioEntry

$ ls src/shared/personalization/studioEntry.ts
src/shared/personalization/studioEntry.ts        # exists; only .ts, no .tsx / .js sibling
```

`../../../../` from `packages/studio/src/components/` reaches the **repo root** — the same depth as the existing
precedent at `VideoStudio.jsx:31-32`:

```
../../../../components/SocialPublishProvider -> <root>/components/SocialPublishProvider
../../../../components/AiAssistantProvider    -> <root>/components/AiAssistantProvider
../../../../src/shared/personalization/studioEntry -> <root>/src/shared/personalization/studioEntry
```

So **the depth is right and the file resolves** — the problem is not the path, it is that the path leaves the
publishable package (§3, §4).

## 3. What `packages/studio` actually publishes

`packages/studio/package.json` (verbatim, relevant fields):

```json
{
  "name": "studio",
  "version": "1.0.0",
  "main": "src/index.js",
  "module": "src/index.js",
  "files": [
    "src",
    "dist"
  ],
  "scripts": {
    "build:css": "tailwindcss -i ./src/tailwind.css -o ./dist/tailwind.css --minify",
    "build": "npm run build:css && babel src --out-dir dist --extensions .js,.jsx"
  },
  "dependencies": {
    "@xyflow/react": "^12.10.2", "axios": "^1.7.0", "lucide-react": "^1.8.0",
    "react-hot-toast": "^2.4.1", "react-icons": "^5.0.1", "react-markdown": "^10.1.0",
    "react-syntax-highlighter": "^16.1.1", "react-toastify": "^11.1.0", "reactflow": "^11.11.4",
    "remark-gfm": "^4.0.1",
    "workflow-builder": "file:../Vibe-Workflow/packages/workflow-builder",
    "ai-agent": "file:../Open-Poe-AI/packages/agents"
  },
  "peerDependencies": { "react": ">=18.0.0", "react-dom": ">=18.0.0" }
}
```

**Answer to "does `main`/`files` exclude `src/`?" — NO. `src/` IS published, and it IS the entry point.**

* `main` and `module` both point at **`src/index.js`** — the *untranspiled, un-bundled source*, not `dist/`.
  `packages/studio/src/index.js` is `"use client"` ESM with JSX and re-exports the studios:
  `export { default as VideoStudio } from './components/VideoStudio';` (line 4).
* `files: ["src", "dist"]` → the published tarball contains **both** the raw source tree and the Babel output.
* **Consequence:** the new import is inside the published artifact. A consumer that installs `studio` from a
  registry gets `node_modules/studio/src/components/VideoStudio.jsx` containing
  `import { PersonalizeStep } from "../../../../src/shared/personalization/studioEntry"`, which resolves to
  `<consumer project root>/src/shared/personalization/studioEntry` — a path that exists **only inside this repo**
  (the root package is `"private": true`, and `src/shared/**` is not in the studio tarball's `files`).
  Since `src/index.js` re-exports `VideoStudio`, `import { VideoStudio } from 'studio'` throws at module-evaluation
  time for any standalone consumer.
* **This is not a new class of defect** — the pre-existing `VideoStudio.jsx:31-32` imports already have the exact
  same shape (`<consumer root>/components/SocialPublishProvider`). The tracked build output proves it:
  `packages/studio/dist/components/VideoStudio.js:18-19` contains
  `require("../../../../components/SocialPublishProvider")` and `require("../../../../components/AiAssistantProvider")`.
  Phase 1 added a **third** instance of a pre-existing broken pattern.
* `packages/studio/src` is otherwise alias-clean: the only `@/`-aliased import sites in the whole package are
  **4 lines, all in `packages/studio/src/components/AppsStudio.test.jsx`** (a test file). The package has **no**
  `next`, `@clerk/nextjs`, `remotion` or `@remotion/*` dependency.

### Bonus finding — `packages/studio/dist` is tracked in git and is STALE

```
$ git ls-files packages/studio/dist | wc -l
29
$ grep -n "PersonalizeStep\|studioEntry" packages/studio/dist/components/VideoStudio.js
(no output — the Phase 1 import is NOT in the tracked dist artifact)
```

`.gitignore` has `dist` (line 11), but 29 `packages/studio/dist/**` files were committed before that rule and are
still tracked. The Phase 1 diff changed `packages/studio/src/components/VideoStudio.jsx` but did **not** regenerate
`dist/`, so the tracked published artifact does not match the source. Running `npm run build:studio` **rewrites
these 29 tracked files** (the build will dirty the working tree).

---

## 4. Is `studioEntry.ts`'s import surface safe for a standalone consumer? — **NO**

`src/shared/personalization/studioEntry.ts` itself imports only relative specifiers (its own doc comment is
accurate about *its own* import list):

```ts
import { createElement, useCallback, useContext, type CSSProperties, type MouseEvent, type ReactNode } from 'react';
import { SmartVideoAccessContext } from '../../access/SmartVideoAccessProvider';
import { ENTITLEMENTS } from '../../access/entitlements';
import { useDemoPersonalize } from './DemoPersonalizeProvider';
import type { StudioTarget } from '../crossStudio';
import type { AssetLibrary, GenerationResult, PersonalizationAccessMode, PersonalizationCompletion, PersonalizationSource } from './types';
```

But the **transitive graph** is app-coupled. Resolved closure from `studioEntry.ts` (local relative + `@/` alias
resolution, 100 local source files reached):

* **20 distinct `@/`-aliased specifiers** — unresolvable by `packages/studio` (its Babel config has no
  module-resolver and no alias):
  `@/access/entitlements`, `@/components/SocialPublishProvider`, `@/data/minimaxH3Demos`, `@/data/motionRecipeCatalog`,
  `@/data/nicheContent`, `@/data/seedance25Demos`, `@/data/types`, `@/lib/authConfig`, `@/lib/muapi`, `@/lib/muapiUpload`,
  `@/packages/studio/src/models.js`, `@/packages/studio/src/muapi`, `@/shared/api/openai`, `@/shared/api/socialCopyService`,
  `@/shared/api/thumbnailService`, `@/shared/crossStudio`, `@/shared/styles/designTokens`, `@/src/shared/api/openaiImage`,
  `@/types/go-ai-viral/prompt`, `@/types/go-ai-viral/seedance`.
* **Next.js-only package specifiers**: `next/navigation` (via `DemoPersonalizeProvider` → `useRouter`) and
  `@clerk/nextjs` (via `src/access/SmartVideoAccessProvider.tsx`). `packages/studio` does not depend on either.
* **Remotion packages not in `packages/studio`'s dependencies**: `remotion`, `@remotion/media`, `@remotion/player`,
  `@remotion/web-renderer`.
* Present in the package: `react` (peer ✓), `lucide-react` (dep ✓).
* **No server-only Node builtins** (`fs`, `next/headers`, `next/server`, `server-only`, `child_process`, …) are
  reached — so this is **not** a server-side hazard. It is a **package-boundary + Next.js-coupling** hazard.
* `studioEntry.ts` is a **`.ts`** file, and the package Babel build handles **only** `.js,.jsx` **inside**
  `packages/studio/src` — so it is neither compiled nor copied into `dist/`.

**Verdict: the import target cannot be satisfied by the standalone package, even if the path were rewritten.**

---

## 5. Recommendation for the cross-package import (for A11 to execute — I did **not** restructure it)

### Verdict: **the current approach is not sound. The button should live in the app root, and the studio package should not import across the package boundary.**

**Reasoning**

1. `packages/studio` is published standalone and publishes its `src/`, so the new import becomes part of a public
   artifact and points at a path that exists only in this repo (§3).
2. The target is not merely mis-pathed — it is app-coupled: 20 `@/` aliases, `next/navigation`, `@clerk/nextjs` and
   Remotion packages that `packages/studio` does not depend on (§4). Even a rewritten path would not be bundleable
   into the standalone package.
3. The repo's own established pattern for exactly this feature runs in the **opposite direction**:
   `components/AiAssistantProvider.tsx` (`AssistStep`) and `components/SocialPublishProvider.tsx` (`PublishStep`)
   live in the **app root** and are consumed by app-root code with `@/components/…`
   (e.g. `src/apps/video-studio/pages/VideoGenerate.tsx:11` → `import { PublishStep } from '@/components/SocialPublishProvider'`).
   The app root then hands the studio its components:
   `components/StandaloneShell.js:24` → `const loadStudio = (name) => dynamic(() => import('studio').then((m) => m[name]), { ssr: false });`
   The **app root owns the package boundary; the package does not import back into the app root.**
   `src/shared/personalization/studioEntry.ts:12-13` even cites these two as "the canonical 'shared modal,
   drop-in button' pattern in this repo" — but the canonical pattern keeps the button in the app root.
4. `VideoStudio.jsx:31-32` (the two pre-existing cross-package imports) is the same defect; Phase 1 followed a
   broken precedent rather than a good one.

### The exact change A11 should make (render-prop injection — no package-boundary crossing)

**Step 1 — new app-root file `components/PersonalizeVideoAction.js`** (app root, so `@/` is fine):

```jsx
"use client";

import { PersonalizeStep } from "@/shared/personalization/studioEntry";

export default function PersonalizeVideoAction({ options, className, children }) {
  return (
    <PersonalizeStep options={options} className={className}>
      {children ?? "Personalize"}
    </PersonalizeStep>
  );
}
```

**Step 2 — `packages/studio/src/components/VideoStudio.jsx`**

* Delete line 35 (`import { PersonalizeStep } from "../../../../src/shared/personalization/studioEntry";`) and the
  explanatory comment on lines 33-34.
* Add an optional prop `renderPersonalizeAction` to the component's destructured props (currently at line ~809).
* Replace the render block at lines 2301-2308

  ```jsx
  <PersonalizeStep
    options={studioPersonalizeOptions}
    className="h-12 px-3 rounded-xl border border-white/10 bg-white/5 hover:bg-white/10 text-[10px] font-bold text-white/70 hover:text-white hover:border-[#22d3ee]/60 transition-colors"
  >
    Personalize
  </PersonalizeStep>
  ```

  with

  ```jsx
  {renderPersonalizeAction?.(studioPersonalizeOptions)}
  ```

  `studioPersonalizeOptions` **already exists** (`VideoStudio.jsx:914-947`) and is exactly the payload
  `PersonalizeStep` needs — so no studio state has to cross the boundary, and the package needs no
  app-root types.

**Step 3 — `components/StandaloneShell.js`** (app root) — where `<VideoStudio … />` is rendered (line 666):

```jsx
import PersonalizeVideoAction from "@/components/PersonalizeVideoAction";
…
<VideoStudio
  apiKey={apiKey}
  droppedFiles={droppedFiles}
  onFilesHandled={handleFilesHandled}
  templateData={templateData}
  locale={locale}
  renderPersonalizeAction={(options) => (
    <PersonalizeVideoAction
      options={options}
      className="h-12 px-3 rounded-xl border border-white/10 bg-white/5 hover:bg-white/10 text-[10px] font-bold text-white/70 hover:text-white hover:border-[#22d3ee]/60 transition-colors"
    >
      Personalize
    </PersonalizeVideoAction>
  )}
/>
```

**Net effect:** `packages/studio` has **zero** app-root imports added by Phase 1; the published tarball is
self-contained; the button keeps the identical DOM, class names and behaviour.

### Alternatives considered and rejected for Wave 1

* **Keep the button inside the package** (re-implement `PersonalizeStep` against a package-local provider) — not
  viable without duplicating the app's shared `DemoPersonalizeProvider` + `PersonalizationModal` host, which is
  exactly what the shared-module design was meant to avoid.
* **Publish the shared module as its own package** (`@openhiggsbolt/personalization`) and add it to
  `packages/studio`'s `dependencies` — architecturally the *right* long-term answer, but a much larger change
  (packaging, versioning, `peerDependencies` for `react`/`next`/`@clerk/nextjs`/`remotion`) and out of Wave 1 scope.
  Worth filing as a follow-up.

### Also required (A11 / orchestrator)

* **`packages/studio/dist` must be regenerated or dropped from the published tarball.** The tracked `dist/` is stale
  (§3). Either commit the regenerated `dist/` (note: this makes the published artifact contain the dangling
  `require("../../../../src/shared/personalization/studioEntry")` unless Step 1-3 above are applied first — so do the
  move **first**), or remove `dist` from `files` (`"files": ["src"]`) and publish only the source, which the
  `main`/`module` fields already imply.
* If Step 1-3 are applied, `packages/studio/src/components/VideoStudio.jsx` regains a clean, alias-free, app-root-free
  import list and the published package becomes self-contained.

---

## Decisions

- **Did not edit the eslint config** — it is not broken. `next/typescript` legitimately enables
  `@typescript-eslint/no-explicit-any` as an error; the 1224 errors are a pre-existing codebase condition
  (proven at the baseline), and the config is not wired into CI at all. Editing it to make the gate green would
  hide a pre-existing debt rather than fix anything, and is explicitly out of scope.
- **Did not edit `packages/studio/src/**`** — the package build is *not* broken by the Phase 1 import (Babel does
  not resolve imports), so the "if and only if the package build genuinely cannot resolve the new import" escape
  clause never triggered. The correct fix is a package-boundary restructure, which the brief assigns to A11.
- **Proved the baseline lint with an isolated pristine tree instead of `git stash push -u`** — the Phase 1 diff is
  *committed* (HEAD == `84a9d1586`, working tree clean of source changes at lint time), so `git stash` had nothing
  to stash. `git archive <baseline> | tar -x` into `/tmp` gives the identical result with zero risk of losing work
  and zero risk to other agents' in-flight edits. I additionally ran the same procedure against HEAD so that both
  sides of the comparison are contaminated-free and byte-comparable.
- **Did not initialise the git submodules** — a large network fetch that mutates the shared worktree and could have
  changed A1's in-flight build. It is the orchestrator's call.
- **Did not run any build command** while `docs/phase2/BUILD_LOCK` was `GRANTED` to A1.
- **Made no source edits at all.** The only file I created is this handoff.

## Interface changes (consumers must know)

- **None made by me.** For A11's recommended change, the consumer-visible surface becomes:
  `packages/studio/src/components/VideoStudio` gains an optional `renderPersonalizeAction(options)` prop
  (additive, default `undefined` → renders nothing, so existing callers are unaffected), and
  `components/StandaloneShell.js` becomes the single injection point.
  `src/shared/personalization/studioEntry.ts` keeps its current public API
  (`useStudioPersonalize`, `PersonalizeStep`, `buildStudioPersonalizeSource`, `assertStudioPersonalizeOptions`).
- **For the orchestrator:** `packages/studio/dist/**` (29 tracked files) is currently stale relative to
  `packages/studio/src/**`; any `npm run build:studio` will rewrite them and dirty the working tree.

## Open questions / blockers

1. **BLOCKER for Gate 2:** `npm run build:packages` was never executed. The orchestrator must:
   `git submodule update --init --recursive && npm run build:packages` (the repo's own `setup` script does this).
   Expect `build:workflow` / `build:agent` to be no-ops-without-the-submodules; the meaningful signal is
   `build:studio` (`npm run build -w studio`).
2. **Open question for the orchestrator:** should `packages/studio/dist` remain in the published tarball at all?
   `main`/`module` point at `src/index.js`, so `dist` is currently redundant in the tarball and is the source of
   the stale-artifact problem.
3. **Open question (not mine, non-blocking):** `npm run lint` has been red repo-wide since before Phase 1 and is not
   in CI. Does the team want a lint gate in CI, and with which rule severities? See the "Recommendation for the
   orchestrator" note under Gate 1.
4. **Open question (not mine, non-blocking):** `VideoStudio.jsx:31-32`'s two pre-existing cross-package imports
   (`SocialPublishProvider`, `AiAssistantProvider`) break a standalone `studio` consumer in exactly the same way.
   Should they be migrated to the same render-prop pattern in a follow-up?
